+ "  - Each stream accepts this ONCE. Biome keeps a stream's event order even for deleted events, so after --unseed the stream reads clean and still refuses a second --seed. `biomestreamtool reset-stream <stream>` is the only way back and it drops that stream's entire history, real events included."
+ "  - Only those exact events. Real events carry their own timestamps and survive."
+ "  - So: seed a VM or a device whose data you do not need. To exercise real behaviour, drive the real producers and read them with --plugin."
+ "  - Synthetic events only cover shapes BiomeStreamCatalog has a factory for, so a branch no shape reaches reads as free rather than as untested."
+ "  - The streams stay readable but do NOT become seedable again: Biome keeps a stream's event order even for deleted events, so the next --seed is refused. `biomestreamtool reset-stream <stream>` is the only way back and it drops that stream's entire history, real events included."

+ "These streams hold no events this query can see and still refuse back-dated writes. That is not a contradiction: `BMFrameStore` keeps a stream's ordering invariant in its segments and a deleted event still counts towards it, so `--unseed` and `biomestreamtool prune` both zero the bytes while leaving the invariant standing — which is exactly the state a stream is in after being unseeded."
+ "This is what an in-use device looks like — anything that wrote to these streams recently, a reboot included, leaves a newest event that a span this wide cannot clear. Seeding is for a device whose streams have been idle for longer than the span, so the usual answers are a fresh VM for another volume arm, or an xctest scenario, which seeds a throwaway store and can be re-run without limit."
